I don't want to waste your time if it's just something everyone deals with.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this flat, heavy feeling?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been a while, I guess. Probably about two years and two months? Give or take? It started right around the time I got put on this big dashboard project at work. It ended up putting me in the spotlight a lot more, which was great, you know, a really good opportunity. I was grateful for it. But I guess that's when I started noticing this feeling. I hope it's not silly that I'm bringing up something from that long ago. Is it normal for things to stick around like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like before this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think so? I mean, I still try to keep up with my hobbies and everything. I still like playing games with my friends online and watching movies, stuff like that. I definitely still get some fun out of it, you know? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping all the time, because I'm not. I try my best to stay positive and be fun to be around. \n\nIs... is it okay that I still enjoy things? Like, does that mean I'm making a bigger deal out of this flat feeling than I should be? I just don't want to be taking up your time if I'm actually doing fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you get as much pleasure from those activities as you used to before this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think it's about the same? I mean, I haven't really noticed a drop in how much fun I have, I don't think. I still laugh at the same stuff and look forward to playing games with my friends. I really try to focus on the good things, you know, so I still get a lot out of it. \n\nI hope that's... is that a helpful answer? I know people usually say you stop liking things when you feel down, so I hope I'm not confusing things by saying I still enjoy my hobbies. I just really want to be accurate for you, and I definitely don't want to make it sound like everything is terrible, because it's really not.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. My sleep has been pretty normal, honestly. I mean, I might be a little tired sometimes, but that's just from work, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary. I try really hard to keep a regular schedule so I'm always ready for the next day. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have anything major to report there? Like, I know sleep problems are usually a big thing when people feel down, so I hope I'm not making this confusing by saying my sleep is fine. Am I doing this right? I don't want to waste your time if it's just something everyone deals with.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this flat, heavy feeling?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been a while, I guess. Probably about two years and two months? Give or take? It started right around the time I got put on this big dashboard project at work. It ended up putting me in the spotlight a lot more, which was great, you know, a really good opportunity. I was grateful for it. But I guess that's when I started noticing this feeling. I hope it's not silly that I'm bringing up something from that long ago. Is it normal for things to stick around like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you used to like before this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think so? I mean, I still try to keep up with my hobbies and everything. I still like playing games with my friends online and watching movies, stuff like that. I definitely still get some fun out of it, you know? I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping all the time, because I'm not. I try my best to stay positive and be fun to be around. \n\nIs... is it okay that I still enjoy things? Like, does that mean I'm making a bigger deal out of this flat feeling than I should be? I just don't want to be taking up your time if I'm actually doing fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you get as much pleasure from those activities as you used to before this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think it's about the same? I mean, I haven't really noticed a drop in how much fun I have, I don't think. I still laugh at the same stuff and look forward to playing games with my friends. I really try to focus on the good things, you know, so I still get a lot out of it. \n\nI hope that's... is that a helpful answer? I know people usually say you stop liking things when you feel down, so I hope I'm not confusing things by saying I still enjoy my hobbies. I just really want to be accurate for you, and I definitely don't want to make it sound like everything is terrible, because it's really not.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. My sleep has been pretty normal, honestly. I mean, I might be a little tired sometimes, but that's just from work, you know? Nothing out of the ordinary. I try really hard to keep a regular schedule so I'm always ready for the next day. \n\nI hope it's okay that I don't have anything major to report there? Like, I know sleep problems are usually a big thing when people feel down, so I hope I'm not making this confusing by saying my sleep is fine. Am I doing this right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you helpful answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since this flat, heavy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, no, my appetite is pretty much exactly the same. I'm eating normally, three meals a day, you know? I haven't been overeating or skipping meals or anything like that. I try to make sure I'm eating well so I have enough energy for work and everything. \n\nIs it... is it weird that my eating habits haven't changed? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out. I don't want to be wasting your time if all my answers are just \"normal.\" I'm really trying to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I try to do my best at work and with my friends, so I don't think I have a lot to feel guilty about? If I make a mistake, I just try to fix it and move on. I don't really sit around blaming myself for things, I don't think. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you're looking for? I know sometimes people get really down on themselves, but I really try to keep a positive outlook and not let things get to me too much. Is it okay that I'm not experiencing that either? Um... wow, days in a row without it? That's... I guess I haven't really kept track like that. I want to say I have good stretches, you know? I really try to.

Um... I definitely wouldn't say severe. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining too much or being dramatic, because I know people have it way worse, and I really do have a lot to be thankful for.

Um... I wouldn't say I have to *force* myself, exactly. I mean, I still do things! I still hang out with friends sometimes, and I try to keep up with my hobbies. I don't want to sound like I've just given up on having fun or anything.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to force yourself to enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I have to *force* myself, exactly. I mean, I still do things! I still hang out with friends sometimes, and I try to keep up with my hobbies. I don't want to sound like I've just given up on having fun or anything. \n\nI guess... maybe it just takes a little more effort than it used to? Like, sometimes I'll agree to go do something, and in the back of my mind I'm kind of just going through the motions, even if it's something I usually like. But I always try to smile and make sure everyone else is having a good time, you know? I don't want to bring anyone else down. \n\nSo, I don't know if \"force\" is the right word... maybe just that the enjoyment doesn't come as naturally as it should? I hope that's an okay way to explain it. I really am trying to stay engaged with things, I promise.\n\n[Last Question]\nHow long do these periods of feeling down and empty usually last when they happen?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / management_analyst / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... wow, how long do they last? I guess... well, it's hard to say exactly. I don't really time it or anything, because I really try not to dwell on it, you know? I want to focus on the positive stuff. 

But if I had to guess... I don't know, it just kind of feels like it's always sort of... there? Like it doesn't really have a clear beginning and end, it just sort of hums along in the background for... a really long time. Weeks, maybe? Or months? I'm honestly not sure if I have a good answer for that. 

I hope that's not frustrating for you? I just... I don't want to make it sound like I'm constantly miserable for months on end, because I'm really not! I still have good moments. It's just that the empty feeling doesn't really fully go away. Is it okay that I don't have an exact timeframe?

I definitely don't want to be the kind of person who just coasts or gives up. \n\nSo I feel like I still have a purpose, and I'm actively trying to move forward and be responsible, even if I'm feeling down. I hope I'm not confusing things by saying no to that one.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, definitely not. I just want to feel better, you know? Not... not end things. \n\nI hope it's a relief to hear that? I know you have to ask, and I'm really glad you're being so thorough. I just want to reassure you that I'm completely safe. Is that... is that the right answer for that one?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your flat, heavy feeling is interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try not to let it affect things. And I was working on MBA planning, but I kind of dropped the ball on that, too. \n\nI feel really bad about it, honestly. I don't want to be someone who lets people down or just gives up on my responsibilities. I'm really trying to get back on track with all of it. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy? I really am trying my best to push through it. Is it normal for things to slip a little when you're feeling like this?\n\nDoctor: How often would you say this flat, heavy feeling occurs during a typical week?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's hard to put an exact number on it, I guess. I really try to have good days, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm just miserable all the time, because I'm really not. \n\nI'd say... maybe it's there a lot of the time? I'm really trying to focus on the positive moments when they happen. It's just that, like I think I mentioned when I first came in, I've been walking around with this flat, heavy feeling for a really long time now. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... a recent day where I felt the least flat... gosh. I really want to give you a good example here, but... my mind is kind of drawing a blank. I'm so sorry, I hope that's not unhelpful.
